SIBLING of it. WON is the door they took; SHOWN is the highest-ranked
sibling we put on the page instead.

ACTA MOBILIER (FR)   regime: ademe_aid
  rank of the won door in the accessible pool: 16/111
------------------------------------------------------------------------------
WON   ademe_aid__etudes_de_faisabilite_des_projets_de_chaufferie__9c6257
  name          Études de faisabilité des projets de chaufferie biomasse
  schemeCode       capitalType grant
  body          ADEME (France)
  window        (none) ((none))  recurrence (none)
  description   Vous souhaitez produire de la chaleur à partir de biomasse (bois) en remplacement d’énergies fossiles ? L’ADEME peut financer une **étude de faisabilité**, indispensable avant tout investissement.

Cette étude permet de :\
– **vérifier la faisabilité technique et économique du projet**,\
– **identifier les solutions techniques adaptées à votre site et structurer le montage du projet** (financier, administratif, juridique).

Elle vous aide à sécuriser votre projet et à faire des choix rentables, durables et adaptés à vos besoins.

Projets liés au dispositif d'aide :
Chauffage au bois

Montant de l'aide ou cout: Jusqu'à 70% du coût de l'étude, dans un maximum de 100 000 €

Contact public pour les questions sur le dispositif: https://fondschaleur.ademe.fr/contact/

Etapes pour activer le dispositif :
Consultez la documentation de l’ADEME pour vérifier l’éligibilité de votre projet ou contactez un conseiller.
Liens liés à l'étape : [Conditions éligibilité et financement](https://agirpourlatransition.ademe.fr/sites/default/files/%C3%89tudes%20en%20faveur%20de%20la%20transition%20%C3%A9cologique%20-%20Conditions%20d%27%C3%A9ligibilit%C3%A9%20et%20de%20financement%20-%202026.pdf)
Choisissez le bureau d'étude énergétique qui va vous accompagner pour mener cette étude de faisabilité et obtenez un devis.
Créez un compte ADEME pour pouvoir déposer votre dossier.
Liens liés à l'étape : [Créer mon compte ADEME](https://agirpourlatransition.ademe.fr/user/register)
Déposez votre dossier : informations administratives, description du projet, détails techniques et financiers, etc. À ce stade, votre projet ne doit pas avoir commencé !
Liens liés à l'étape : [Déposer mon dossier](https://agirpourlatransition.ademe.fr/entreprises/aides-financieres/catalogue/2026/etudes-des-projets-de-chaufferie-biomasse?cible=79)
L'ADEME étudie votre demande d'aide. Si votre demande est acceptée, recevez un contrat de financement précisant le montant d’aide maximum, les conditions et les modalités de versement.
Réalisez votre étude pour vérifier la faisabilité technique et économique de votre projet d'implantation d'une chaufferie biomasse.


Lien externe de présentation de l'aide https://agirpourlatransition.ademe.fr/entreprises/aides-financieres/catalogue/2026/etudes-des-projets-de-chaufferie-biomasse?cible=79
  eligibility   Eligibilité Sectorielle : Tous secteurs d’activité
Secteurs principalement concernés par le dispositif : A|B|C|D|E|G|H|I
Eligibilité en fonction du nombre de salariés: Toutes tailles

 Autres conditions d'éligibilité :
- L'opération pour laquelle vous sollicitez une aide financière ne doit pas avoir commencé ou ne doit pas avoir donné lieu à des engagements fermes (sous quelque forme que ce soit : marché signé, commande signée, devis accepté…) Périmètre géographique (codes catalogue) : PAYS-99100. Fenêtre de candidature : 2022-12-31 à sans date de clôture.

WON   ademe_aid__prototypes_de_l_extreme_defi_741ff5
  name          Prototypes de l'eXtrême Défi
  schemeCode       capitalType grant
  body          ADEME (France)
  window        (none) ((none))  recurrence (none)
  description   L’eXtrême Défi Mobilité est un parcours d'innovation visant à imaginer et développer des solutions de déplacement durables et adaptées aux territoires dépendants de l’automobile.

- Participez à des **sessions de créativité** pour concevoir de nouveaux concepts d'objets roulants.

- Prototypez des véhicules, avec des **expérimentations continues** auprès d'utilisateurs dans les territoires partenaires.

- Passez à la **phase d’industrialisation** : production, assemblage, réparation, et reconditionnement des véhicules tout en affinant le design.

- Identifiez les **sources de financement** régionales, nationales ou de l’ADEME pour soutenir les différentes phases de votre projet.

- Bénéficiez des nombreuses **expertises du réseau** : mécanique, design, électronique, urbanisme, ecoconception, modèles économiques, communication, marketing, etc.

Projets liés au dispositif d'aide :
Changement des modes de transport
Cyclologistique
Écoconduite
Achat d'un véhicule intermédiaire

Montant de l'aide ou cout: Gratuit

Durée de l'aide: Jusqu'à 3 ans d'accompagnement

Contact public pour les questions sur le dispositif: https://xd.ademe.fr/autres-questions

Etapes pour activer le dispositif :
Découvrez le programme eXtrême Défi.
Liens liés à l'étape : [Le site de l’eXtrême Défi](https://xd.ademe.fr/approche-integrale)
Explorez les différentes manières de vous impliquer dans le programme.
Liens liés à l'étape : [Quatre niveaux d'engagement possibles](https://wikixd.fabmob.io/wiki/Devenir_acteur_de_l'XD)
Exprimer votre intérêt pour rejoindre la communauté de l'eXtrême Défi.
Liens liés à l'étape : [Rejoignez l'extrême Défi](https://airtable.com/apprjFOnbLySO8spa/shraH0xKsFZnkZOKw)

SHOWN ademe_aid__soutenir_le_tri_a_la_source_et_la_valorisation_d_b0b630
  name          Soutenir le tri à la source et la valorisation des biodéchets
  schemeCode       capitalType grant
  body          ADEME (France)
  window        (none) ((none))  recurrence (none)
  description   Vous cherchez à mieux gérer vos **biodéchets** et à en faire une ressource ? La CCI Hauts-de-France vous accompagne pour identifier les solutions de réduction et de valorisation adaptées à votre activité. Cet accompagnement vous aide à structurer une démarche concrète et efficace.

- Analysez la **nature, le volume et la fréquence** de vos biodéchets, ainsi que leur localisation.

- Repérez les solutions de **valorisation proches de chez vous** : compostage, méthanisation, etc.

- Référencez vos flux sur une **plateforme géolocalisée** dédiée pour faciliter les synergies locales.

- Bénéficiez d’un **outil d’aide à la décision** pour choisir les solutions techniques, repérer les dispositifs de financement mobilisables, et entrer en contact avec les bons interlocuteurs.

Projets liés au dispositif d'aide :
Gestion des biodéchets
Zéro perte en production

Montant de l'aide ou cout: De 750 à 3 500 € HT

Durée de l'aide: De 1 à 5 jours selon la taille et le secteur d'activité de l'entreprise

Contact public pour les questions sur le dispositif: https://mission-transition-ecologique.beta.gouv.fr/aides-entreprise/gestion-des-biodechets?utm_campaign=openData

Etapes pour activer le dispositif :
Contactez un conseiller via le formulaire dédié pour exprimer votre intérêt.
Liens liés à l'étape : [mission transition écologique](https://mission-transition-ecologique.beta.gouv.fr/aides-entreprise/gestion-des-biodechets?utm_campaign=openData)
Un conseiller vous rappelle pour répondre à vos questions et vous guider dans la constitution de votre demande.
Définissez avec l’expert le périmètre du diagnostic, la méthode et le calendrier.
Lancez le diagnostic avec l'expert proposé par votre CCI.


Lien externe de présentation de l'aide https://hautsdefrance.cci.fr/solutions/accompagnement-gestion-biodechets/
  eligibility   Eligibilité Sectorielle : Entreprises produisant des biodéchets (industries agro-alimentaires, Hotellerie restauration, Grande & moyennes surfaces...)
Secteurs principalement concernés par le dispositif : A|E|G|I
Eligibilité en fonction du nombre de salariés: Toutes tailles
Aires géographiques éligibles : Hauts-de-France Périmètre géographique (codes catalogue) : REG-32.

WON   ademe_aid__audit_energetique_en_industrie_54a6fb
  name          Audit énergétique en industrie
  schemeCode       capitalType grant
  body          ADEME (France)
  window        2026-12-31 (call_deadline)  recurrence (none)
  description   L’ADEME vous aide à financer un audit énergétique sur le périmètre de votre choix : site de production, atelier ou process.

L’audit énergétique vous permet :

-  **de réaliser un état des lieux complet de vos consommations**, pour mieux comprendre où et comment l’énergie est utilisée ;
-  **d’identifier les postes les plus énergivores**, en croisant les données techniques et économiques ;
- **de définir et hiérarchiser les actions les plus efficaces**, selon leur rentabilité, leur temps de retour sur investissement et leur facilité de mise en œuvre.

Projets liés au dispositif d'aide :
Audit énergétique
Management de l’énergie

Montant de l'aide ou cout: Jusqu'à 80% du coût de l'étude, dans un maximum de 100 000 €

Contact public pour les questions sur le dispositif: https://agirpourlatransition.ademe.fr/form/contact?id_dsp=DSP000852

Etapes pour activer le dispositif :
Consultez la documentation de l’ADEME pour vérifier l’éligibilité de votre projet ou contactez un conseiller.
Liens liés à l'étape : [Conditions éligibilité et financement](https://agirpourlatransition.ademe.fr/sites/default/files/PACTE%20Entreprises%20-%20Audit%20%C3%A9nerg%C3%A9tique%20-%20Conditions%20d%27%C3%A9ligibilit%C3%A9%20et%20de%20financement%20-%202026.pdf)
Choisissez un auditeur énergétique "Reconnu Garant de l'Environnement" (RGE), et obtenez un devis.
Liens liés à l'étape : [Annuaire des professionnels RGE](https://france-renov.gouv.fr/annuaires-professionnels/artisan-rge-architecte)
Créez un compte ADEME pour pouvoir déposer votre dossier.
Liens liés à l'étape : [Créer mon compte ADEME](https://agirpourlatransition.ademe.fr/user/register)
Déposez votre dossier : informations administratives, description du projet, détails techniques et financiers, etc. À ce stade, votre projet ne doit pas avoir commencé !
Liens liés à l'étape : [Déposer mon dossier](https://agirpourlatransition.ademe.fr/entreprises/aides-financieres/catalogue/2026/pacte-entreprises-audit-energetique-volontaire-en-industrie)
L'ADEME étudie votre demande d'aide. Si votre demande est acceptée, recevez un contrat de financement précisant le montant d’aide maximum, les conditions et les modalités de versement.
Réalisez votre audit énergétique. Vous disposerez d'un état des lieux complet de vos consommations, des pistes d'amélioration sous forme de plan d'action ainsi que les gains économiques associés et leurs temps de retour sur investissement.


Lien externe de présentation de l'aide https://agirpourlatransition.ademe.fr/entreprises/aides-financieres/catalogue/2026/pacte-entreprises-audit-energetique-volontaire-en-industrie
  eligibility   Eligibilité Sectorielle : Industrie
Secteurs principalement concernés par le dispositif : A|B|C|D|E
Eligibilité en fonction du nombre de salariés: Toutes tailles

 Autres conditions d'éligibilité :
- L'opération pour laquelle vous sollicitez une aide financière ne doit pas avoir commencé ou ne doit pas avoir donné lieu à des engagements fermes (sous quelque forme que ce soit : marché signé, commande signée, devis accepté…)
- Éligible pour les sites de moins de 250 salariés des grandes entreprises, dès lors que l’entreprise est à jour de ses obligations vis-à-vis de l’audit énergétique réglementaire
- Ne sont pas éligibles : les sites de moins de 250 salariés des grandes entreprises dès lors que l'échéance de renouvellement de l'audit énergétique réglementaire est inférieure à 1 an, ainsi que les locaux d’activité tertiaire seuls. Périmètre géographique (codes catalogue) : PAYS-99100. Fenêtre de candidature : 2022-12-31 à sans date de clôture.

Le focus plastique « ORPLAST-régénération » est soutenu par le plan de relance. Le recyclage de déchets vise à préparer des matières premières de recyclage (MPR). Il présente de multiples atouts : en évitant l’extraction et la transformation inutiles de ressources naturelles, il réduit la consommation d’énergie, les émissions de gaz à effet de serre et les autres impacts environnementaux des filières industrielles. Pourtant, les matériaux utilisés dans la fabrication de nos produits contiennent

Embedded Acoustics BV (NL)   regime: edf
  rank of the won door in the accessible pool: 17/48
------------------------------------------------------------------------------
WON   edf_da
  name          Edf DA
  schemeCode    (none)   capitalType grant
  body          European Commission
  window        (none) ((none))  recurrence (none)
  description   Part-funded collaborative defence development that turns an existing design into hardware - prototyping, testing, qualification and certification of a defence product - where the EU pays only a slice of the cost and the consortium and its Member States carry the rest.
  eligibility   WHAT MAKES A DEVELOPMENT ACTION DIFFERENT FROM ITS ONLY SIBLING, THE RESEARCH ACTION:

1. YOU DO NOT GET 100%. Development actions are the only EDF leaf where the EU pays a fraction of eligible costs, and the fraction depends on which activity you are doing. Table 1 baseline rates: integrating knowledge up to 65%, studies up to 90%, design up to 65%, "The system prototyping of a defence Up to 20%", testing up to 45%, "The qualification of a defence product, Up to 70%", certification up to 70%, increasing efficiency up to 65%. Table 3 maxima after bonuses: prototyping up to 55%, testing/qualification/certification up to 80%, integrating knowledge / studies / design / increasing efficiency up to 100%. So the closer you get to a fieldable product, the less the EU pays - prototyping at a 20% baseline is the single sharpest number in the regime.

2. THE BONUS MACHINERY EXISTS ONLY HERE: "Table 2. Increase of funding rates (bonus) for development actions" - PESCO bonus +10%, SME bonus (proportion of eligible costs to EU SMEs, up to 5% for non-cross-border SMEs plus twice the proportion for cross-border SMEs), mid-cap bonus +10%. The overall increase cannot exceed 35% of the total eligible costs of the activity, and the result cannot exceed the Table 3 maxima. Derogation: "the activities carried out under the specific actions that target disruptive technologies under the EDF-2026-LS-DIS call and aim to develop technologies or assets that increase efficiency across the life cycle of defence products and technologies (Article 10.3(i) of the EDF Regulation) will be funded at 100%."

3. ACTIVITY TYPES ARE THE MATURITY GATE. System prototyping, testing, qualification, certification and increasing efficiency are eligible ONLY for development actions. Generating knowledge is NOT eligible for a development action at all - a DA proposal cannot be framed as new knowledge creation.

4. THE NO-PROFIT DEROGATION IS DA-SPECIFIC: "No profit rule: To ensure the continuity of the development actions after the period of EU financing provided for in the grant, potential revenue to be generated by these actions will not be taken into consideration in accordance with point (a) of Article 195(3) of the Financial Regulation."

5. TOPIC/CALL-LEVEL DA CONDITIONS (not all development actions):
- EDF-2026-DA-EXP (expedited): "Proposals should be submitted within a shorter timeframe than for EDF-2026-DA, and the evaluation process should be faster." EDF-2026-DA-ACC (accelerated): same submission timeframe, faster evaluation.
- EDF-2026-LS-DA-DIS: consortium of at least two legal entities in at least two different Member States or EDF associated countries, at least two of which are not controlled by the same legal entity and do not control each other - a lower floor than the standard Article 10(4) rule.

IMPORTANT NEGATIVE FINDING: the prior Member State commitment to procure / joint procurement, which is the textbook DA discriminator, is NOT written into the 2026 (or 2025 Part 2) work programme. The work programme delegates eligibility to "Articles 9 and 10(4) of the EDF Regulation" and uses that same phrase for the RA calls. Do not seed a procurement-commitment gate from this source.
------------------------------------------------------------------------------
SHOWN edf_ra
  name          Edf RA
  schemeCode    (none)   capitalType grant
  body          European Commission
  window        (none) ((none))  recurrence (none)
  description   Fully EU-funded collaborative defence R&D that stops before anything is built: knowledge generation, feasibility studies and design for defence products and technologies, delivered by a cross-border consortium of EU/associated-country entities.
  eligibility   WHAT MAKES A RESEARCH ACTION DIFFERENT FROM ITS ONLY SIBLING, THE DEVELOPMENT ACTION:

1. FUNDING RATE: research actions are funded at 100% of eligible costs for every activity type open to them, with no bonus machinery and no cap below 100%. In Table 1 (baseline) and Table 3 (maximum) the research-action column is 100% throughout, while the development-action column is capped: e.g. "Studies such as feasibility studies to explore 100% Up to 90%" and design "100% Up to 65%". The bonus table is explicitly headed "Table 2. Increase of funding rates (bonus) for development actions" - PESCO, SME and mid-cap bonuses are not available to a research action because it is already at 100%.

2. ACTIVITY TYPES. "The table below indicates which activities are eligible for research actions and which for development actions." Generating knowledge (activity (a)) is eligible for research actions and NOT eligible for development actions - it is the only RA-exclusive activity. Conversely system prototyping, testing, qualification, certification and increasing efficiency are not eligible under a research action at all, so an RA proposal may cover only generating knowledge, integrating knowledge, studies and design.

3. THE NO-PROFIT DEROGATION DOES NOT APPLY. The work programme's no-profit rule is written to "ensure the continuity of the development actions" only; RA revenue treatment is unchanged.

4. TOPIC-LEVEL RA CONDITIONS (do not apply to all research actions - flagged honestly):
- EDF-2026-LS-RA-SI (2 topics): "The proposals must build upon or integrate results that have been achieved within one or several projects that were funded under an EU programme call with a focus on civil applications." Applicants must confirm they have or will have the rights to use and commercialise those prior results. This is a spin-in-from-civil gate with no development-action equivalent.
- EDF-2026-LS-DIS-RA-SMERO-NT (1 topic): "Members of the consortium need to be SMEs (as defined in Commission Recommendation 2003/361/EC) or research organisations. The coordinator of the consortium needs to be an SME. The budget allocated to research organisations cannot exceed 40% of the total requested grant amount."

PUT IN NEITHER LEAF because the 2026 work programme applies them identically to RA and DA: the consortium rule ("Any eligible consortium as defined in Articles 9 and 10(4) of the EDF Regulation" is the wording used for the RA calls AND the DA calls), non-EU ownership/effective-control conditions, the Member-State rights-to-use clause, the 25% flat indirect-cost rate, and TRL floors (topic-level on both sides: TRL 4 for both the RA and the DA non-thematic SME topics).

IKAA may be contributed by constituent and/or affiliated entities of the private members only. Contributing partners and their affiliated entities cannot contribute IKAA. Participants must – up to four years after the end of the action (see Data Sheet, Point 1) – use their best efforts to ensure that resulting health technologies and services will be broadly available and accessible, as soon as possible and at fair and reasonable conditions. the participants must (if requested by the granting authority) grant non-exclusive licences - under fair and reasonable conditions - to their results to legal entities that commit to rapidly and broadly exploiting the resulting health technologies and services In case of transfer of the ownership or licensing of results, participants must pass on such additional exploitation obligations to the legal entities exploiting the results. For up to four years after the action (see Data Sheet, Point 1), the funding body must be informed every year about the status of the development of the product and any other exploitation of the results through an annual report that is due on each anniversary of the end of the grant agreement. Studies must be registered in a registry meeting WHO Registry criteria37 before recruitment of the first subject. Proposals must comply with all ethics requirements arising out of the research, in line with Article 112 of the Council Regulation 2021/208556. Global Health EDCTP3 may, up to four (4) years after the end of the action, object to a transfer of ownership or to the exclusive licensing of results, as set out in the specific provision of Annex 5
------------------------------------------------------------------------------
SHOWN eurohpc
  name          Eurohpc
  schemeCode    (none)   capitalType grant
  body          European High Performance Computing Joint Undertaking (EuroHPC JU)
  window        (none) ((none))  recurrence (none)
  description   The Joint Undertaking provides financial support in the form of procurement or research and innovation grants to participants following open and competitive calls.
  eligibility   For all activities implemented by the EuroHPC JU that are funded from the Connecting Europe Facility (CEF) budget, the Governing Board may decide to limit in the calls for proposals or procurements the eligibility of participants according to Connecting Europe Facility Article 11(4). For all activities implemented by the EuroHPC JU that are funded from the Digital Europe Programme (DEP) budget, the Governing Board may decide to limit in the calls for proposals or procurements the eligibility of participants according to Digital Europe Articles 12(6) and 18(4). For all activities implemented by the EuroHPC JU that are funded from the Horizon Europe (HE) budget, the Governing Board may decide to limit in the calls for proposals the eligibility of participants according to Horizon Europe Article 22(5). participation is limited to legal entities established in Member States that are members of the EuroHPC Joint Undertaking or Participating States Norway and Iceland. Proposals including entities established in countries outside the scope specified in the call/topic/action will be ineligible. Participants should be citizens of any of the EuroHPC Participating States and perform their work in any of the Participating States. The prize will be granted to individuals, not to institutions or companies, and the results will be made available as open source / public domain. provide SMEs and start-ups with the full range of additional activities necessary to develop and support the AI ecosystem The overall administrative budget for 2026 remains well below the maximum ceiling foreseen under the EuroHPC JU Regulation of EUR 92 Million for the entire 2021-2027 Multi-Annual Financial Framework. An EU contribution of EUR 48.6 Million (50% of total funding) will be matched by a PS contribution of EUR 48.6 Million (50% of total funding). The maximum price payable under this contract is set at EUR 1.8 Million. The prize budget is 300 000 EUR (indicative). The first three ranked solutions will share the prize, with amounts depending on the challenge addressed. Type of Action Grant, Horizon Europe. The call will be implemented in two stages: In a first step, applicants will be selected on the basis of a reference implementation, the anticipated quantum advantage, potential impact and other criteria. Successful applicants will be awarded access to EuroHPC quantum computers to develop and verify the proposed implementation. Type of Action Procurement A key aspect is to ensure that all the IP necessary to produce the solutions remains in the EU, effectively creating an independent European source of critical technology. With this prize the EU intends to incentivise young researchers, inventors and entrepreneurs to develop an application demonstrating a path towards quantum advantage, addressing a concrete problem. Grants and procurements can be linked with another grant funded from any other EU funding programme including the Recovery and Resilience Fund, provided that there is no double funding and that such support does not cover the same cost. Calls to be launched in 2026 will be prepared by the JU and presented for adoption by the Governing Board by separate Governing Board Decisions. Grants and procurements can be linked with another grant funded from any other EU funding programme including the Recovery and Resilience Fund, provided that there is no double funding and that such support does not cover the same cost. The grants under both calls will be managed as linked actions. the results will be made available as open source / public domain For EuroHPC JU projects funded under the H2020 and the HE programmes, ex-post audits are carried out since 2022 by the Common Audit Service of the European Commission, and as such will continue in 2026.

AALTO KORKEAKOULUSAATIO SR (FI)   regime: horizon_eu_pillar_ii
  rank of the won door in the accessible pool: 13/36
------------------------------------------------------------------------------
WON   erc_poc
  name          ERC POC
  schemeCode    (none)   capitalType grant
  body          European Research Council Executive Agency
  window        (none) ((none))  recurrence (none)
  description   A EUR 150 000, 18-month lump sum that lets a current or recent ERC frontier-research grant holder test whether one specific result from that grant can become a product, a spin-out or a licence - you cannot apply unless you already hold, or recently held, the ERC grant it derives from.
  eligibility   ERC Proof of Concept is gated on an ERC grant the applicant already holds, which no other instrument in this programme requires. "The ERC Proof of Concept Grant is open to Principal Investigators whose ERC main frontier research grant is ongoing, or Principal Investigators whose ERC main frontier research grant ends on or after 1 January 2025, subject to restrictions provided in Annex 3 below." There is no route in for a company or a researcher without an ERC main grant.

The idea must also derive from that grant. "These grants are available only to Principal Investigators, whose proposals draw substantially on their ERC-funded research; therefore, applicants need to demonstrate the relation between the idea to be taken to proof of concept and their main grant." The same test is the award criterion: "The activities of the Proof of Concept Grant shall draw substantially on the research carried out as part of the main ERC frontier research grant."

The award and its duration are fixed rather than proposed. It is awarded as a single lump sum contribution of EUR 150 000 "for a period of eighteen months". Extensions may be granted when duly justified. The lump sum covers direct and indirect eligible costs and is paid regardless of costs actually incurred. Indicative call budget EUR 60 000 000.

A quota applies per parent grant. "A maximum of three Proof of Concept Grants may be awarded for a Starting, Consolidator, or Advanced Grant project, and a maximum of six Proof of Concept Grants - for a Synergy Grant project." Grants may run in parallel. A Synergy Principal Investigator may apply "only with the written consent of all Principal Investigators of the same Synergy Grant project", and each may submit one application. A Principal Investigator may submit only one proposal to this call under this Work Programme; two cut-off rounds run in the year and only the first admissible and eligible proposal is evaluated.

The applicant is a single Principal Investigator, not a consortium. "Proposals for the Proof of Concept Grant call are submitted by the Principal Investigator on behalf of the Host Institution", and a binding Host Institution Support Letter must be supplied at the time of application or the proposal may be declared inadmissible. Consortium agreements are not required, and "Excellence is the sole criterion of evaluation." A Principal Investigator rejected for a breach of research integrity under Work Programme 2024 or Work Programme 2025 may not apply.
------------------------------------------------------------------------------
SHOWN horizon_eu_miss
  name          Horizon EU Miss
  schemeCode    (none)   capitalType grant
  body          (none)
  window        (none) ((none))  recurrence (none)
  description   Grants that implement five named EU Missions — Adaptation to Climate Change, Cancer, 100 Climate-Neutral and Smart Cities, Restore our Ocean and Waters, and A Soil Deal for Europe — funding demonstration and replication in real territories, where the cities, regions or living labs are beneficiaries of the grant rather than study subjects.
  eligibility   The Missions work programme covers all five EU Missions — "Adaptation to Climate Change", "Cancer", "100 Climate-Neutral and Smart cities by 2030", "Restore our Ocean and Waters by 2030" and "A Soil Deal for Europe" — plus joint calls between them. Its discriminating conditions are membership conditions: the beneficiary must be, or must come from, a place that has already joined the Mission. The Cities Mission and its joint calls repeatedly require: "At least three cities, represented by a local authority or by an entity with an explicit consent from the local authority, each from a different Member State or Associated Country, must participate as beneficiaries." and "At least one of the three cities must be one of the 112 cities selected for the EU Mission on Climate-Neutral and Smart Cities". A two-city variant exists, and a lead/follower variant: "At least three lead cities, represented by a local authority or by an entity with an explicit consent from the local authority, from different Member States or Associated Countries along with at least three follower cities, must participate ensuring a good geographical balance." Joint Cities/Adaptation topics add a charter test: "Entities from at least four cities, each from a different Member State or Associated Country, must participate as beneficiaries." and "at least one must be a signatory to the Adaptation Mission Charter" — the Charter being the instrument by which, per this document, "more than 325 regional and local authorities have committed to working together to transition faster to a climate resilient Europe." Demonstration geography is also prescribed: "Demonstration activities must take place in the territory of at least 3 different regional or local authorities that host cultural heritage, each established in a different Member State or Associated Country." and, on a cross-border topic, "The demonstration areas must span adjacent regions on both sides of a national border." The Soil Mission carries CL6-style conditions: "Proposals must apply the multi-actor approach. See definition of the multi-actor approach in the introduction to this work programme part." on 9 topic pages, plus a biogeographic constraint — "regions: Alpine or Atlantic, i.e., the majority of the living labs of each proposal must be located in one of these two biogeographical regions." — and living-lab cascade funding: "Beneficiaries may provide financial support to third parties to facilitate active involvement of smaller actors (e.g. land managers and owners such as farmers, SMEs or civil societies) in one or more of the living labs of the project." One Adaptation topic makes cascade funding compulsory and unusually large: "Beneficiaries must provide financial support to third parties. The support to third parties can only be provided in the form of grants. The maximum amount to be granted to each third party is EUR 800,000" — the only 'must' FSTP clause in the part, against 14 'may' clauses.

==============================================================================

SHOWN horizon_eu_cl1
  name          Horizon EU CL1
  schemeCode    (none)   capitalType grant
  body          (none)
  window        (none) ((none))  recurrence (none)
  description   Collaborative R&I grants for health research that generates clinical evidence — disease prevention and treatment, vaccines and medical countermeasures, health-system transformation, and the tools and cohorts behind them — for consortia that usually run or plan clinical studies.
  eligibility   Cluster 1 Health's discriminating condition runs the opposite way from the security clusters: it widens the eligible-country set rather than narrowing it. On 37 distinct pages the Eligibility conditions row states "In recognition of the opening of the US National Institutes of Health’s programmes to European researchers, any legal entity established in the United States of America is eligible to receive Union funding." On Coordination and Support Action topics the same reciprocity is granted in a narrower form — US entities "may exceptionally participate as a beneficiary or affiliated entity, and are eligible to receive Union funding" — paired with a derogation from the standard consortium rule in the other direction: "Coordinators of projects must be legal entities established in an EU Member State or Associated Country." No other Pillar II cluster opens funding eligibility to US entities as a standing rule. Second, the cluster is organised around clinical evidence generation and defines its own unusually broad object: "In the context of the Health Cluster Work Programme part for 2026-2027, a clinical study covers clinical studies/trials/investigations/cohorts and is defined as any systematic prospective or retrospective collection and analysis of health data obtained from individual patients or healthy persons" — explicitly wider than Regulation 536/2014, and topics repeatedly note that "As proposals under this topic are expected to include clinical studies, the use of the template is strongly encouraged", the template being a dedicated clinical-studies annex in the submission system. Third, many topics carry a binding SSH requirement: "This topic requires the effective contribution of social sciences and humanities (SSH) disciplines and the involvement of SSH experts". Fourth, the co-funded European Partnership topics derogate from the standard funding rates: "The funding rate is 50% of the eligible costs." (Rare Diseases) and "The funding rate is 30% of the eligible costs." (Personalised Medicine). Notably, unlike Clusters 3 and 4, this part states no TRL in any topic conditions table (zero TRL rows across 211 pages), and Article 22(5) participation limits appear on only one topic (page 173), so an applicant's country eligibility here is broader than in the security or space clusters, not tighter.

==============================================================================

SHOWN horizon_eu_cl6
  name          Horizon EU CL6
  schemeCode    (none)   capitalType grant
  body          (none)
  window        (none) ((none))  recurrence (none)
  description   Collaborative research and innovation grants for food systems, farming, forestry, fisheries and aquaculture, biodiversity, soil, water, the circular bioeconomy and rural/coastal/urban communities — where on most topics farmers, foresters, fishers, advisors and other end-users must sit inside the consortium as co-creators, not as an advisory board.
  eligibility   Cluster 6's discriminating condition is the multi-actor approach, and it is an eligibility condition, not an ambition. 40 of the 69 topic conditions tables carry "The proposals must apply the multi-actor approach. See definition of the multi-actor approach in this work programme part." The definition, set out in a dedicated section 'Specific conditions for multi-actor projects', binds the consortium's composition and method: "A multi-actor project ensures the genuine and sufficient involvement of a targeted array of actors in co-creation, which serves the objectives of the project proposal." Topic texts translate this into named practitioner classes: "Proposals must implement the multi-actor approach, with a consortium based on a balanced mix of relevant actors with complementary knowledge clearly activating advisors, farmers and/or foresters and/or rural actors." It also imposes a deliverable no sibling cluster requires: "the new knowledge and innovation generated by the multi-actor projects must be summarised in an appropriate number of ‘practice abstracts’ in the common EIP-AGRI format for Horizon". Second, a set of place-based topics require named territorial demonstrators inside the consortium as beneficiaries. Living-lab topics: "at least five (5) distinct ‘living labs’ linked to regional or local authorities (and possibly together with their public and/or private ecosystem actors operating in their respective territories) must be part of the consortium as beneficiaries, among which at least two (2) living labs must be from different demonstration sites and another three (3) from other and different replication sites. 1 out of the 3 replication sites must be located in Horizon Europe widening countries (including Outermost Regions)." A rural/urban replication topic goes further: "at least nine (9) distinct regional or local authorities must be part of the consortium as beneficiaries, among which at least three (3) from different demonstration cities/regions and another six (6) from other and different replication cities/regions." Third, geographic derogations are common: "due to the specific challenge of this topic, in addition to the minimum number of participants set out in the General Annexes, consortia must include at least three independent legal entities established in an African Union member state"; "Due to the scope of this topic, legal entities established in Brazil are exceptionally eligible for Union funding."; "All international organisations are exceptionally eligible for funding."; and on one topic "the participation of at least one research institution or relevant public authority from Ukraine in the consortium is mandatory." Fourth, two partnership-continuation topics collapse the consortium rule to a single named applicant, e.g. "The proposal must be submitted by the coordinator of the consortium funded under HORIZON-CL6-2022-GOVERNANCE-01-02 and HORIZON-CL6-2024-GOVERNANCE-01-1: European Partnership for a climate neutral, sustainable and productive Blue Economy." These are effectively closed to new entrants.

"A maximum of three Proof of Concept Grants may be awarded for a Starting, Consolidator, or Advanced Grant project, and a maximum of six Proof of Concept Grants - for a Synergy Grant project." Grants may run in parallel. A Synergy Principal Investigator may apply "only with the written consent of all Principal Investigators of the same Synergy Grant project", and each may submit one application. A Principal Investigator may submit only one proposal to this call under this Work Programme; two cut-off rounds run in the year and only the first admissible and eligible proposal is evaluated.

The applicant is a single Principal Investigator, not a consortium. "Proposals for the Proof of Concept Grant call are submitted by the Principal Investigator on behalf of the Host Institution", and a binding Host Institution Support Letter must be supplied at the time of application or the proposal may be declared inadmissible. Consortium agreements are not required, and "Excellence is the sole criterion of evaluation." A Principal Investigator rejected for a breach of research integrity under Work Programme 2024 or Work Programme 2025 may not apply.

==============================================================================
ASOCIACION EMPRESARIAL DE INVESTIGACION CENTRO TECNOLOGICO DEL CALZADOY DEL PLASTICO DE LA REGION DE MURCIA (ES)   regime: horizon_eu_pillar_ii
  rank of the won door in the accessible pool: 26/107
------------------------------------------------------------------------------
WON   horizon_eu_cl6
  name          Horizon EU CL6
  schemeCode    (none)   capitalType grant
  body          (none)
  window        (none) ((none))  recurrence (none)
  description   Collaborative research and innovation grants for food systems, farming, forestry, fisheries and aquaculture, biodiversity, soil, water, the circular bioeconomy and rural/coastal/urban communities — where on most topics farmers, foresters, fishers, advisors and other end-users must sit inside the consortium as co-creators, not as an advisory board.
  eligibility   Cluster 6's discriminating condition is the multi-actor approach, and it is an eligibility condition, not an ambition. 40 of the 69 topic conditions tables carry "The proposals must apply the multi-actor approach. See definition of the multi-actor approach in this work programme part." The definition, set out in a dedicated section 'Specific conditions for multi-actor projects', binds the consortium's composition and method: "A multi-actor project ensures the genuine and sufficient involvement of a targeted array of actors in co-creation, which serves the objectives of the project proposal." Topic texts translate this into named practitioner classes: "Proposals must implement the multi-actor approach, with a consortium based on a balanced mix of relevant actors with complementary knowledge clearly activating advisors, farmers and/or foresters and/or rural actors." It also imposes a deliverable no sibling cluster requires: "the new knowledge and innovation generated by the multi-actor projects must be summarised in an appropriate number of ‘practice abstracts’ in the common EIP-AGRI format for Horizon". Second, a set of place-based topics require named territorial demonstrators inside the consortium as beneficiaries. Living-lab topics: "at least five (5) distinct ‘living labs’ linked to regional or local authorities (and possibly together with their public and/or private ecosystem actors operating in their respective territories) must be part of the consortium as beneficiaries, among which at least two (2) living labs must be from different demonstration sites and another three (3) from other and different replication sites. 1 out of the 3 replication sites must be located in Horizon Europe widening countries (including Outermost Regions)." A rural/urban replication topic goes further: "at least nine (9) distinct regional or local authorities must be part of the consortium as beneficiaries, among which at least three (3) from different demonstration cities/regions and another six (6) from other and different replication cities/regions." Third, geographic derogations are common: "due to the specific challenge of this topic, in addition to the minimum number of participants set out in the General Annexes, consortia must include at least three independent legal entities established in an African Union member state"; "Due to the scope of this topic, legal entities established in Brazil are exceptionally eligible for Union funding."; "All international organisations are exceptionally eligible for funding."; and on one topic "the participation of at least one research institution or relevant public authority from Ukraine in the consortium is mandatory." Fourth, two partnership-continuation topics collapse the consortium rule to a single named applicant, e.g. "The proposal must be submitted by the coordinator of the consortium funded under HORIZON-CL6-2022-GOVERNANCE-01-02 and HORIZON-CL6-2024-GOVERNANCE-01-1: European Partnership for a climate neutral, sustainable and productive Blue Economy." These are effectively closed to new entrants.
------------------------------------------------------------------------------
SHOWN horizon_eu_cbe
  name          Horizon EU CBE
  schemeCode    (none)   capitalType grant
  body          Circular Bio-based Europe Joint Undertaking
  window        (none) ((none))  recurrence (none)
  description   Grants of roughly EUR 1.2m to EUR 20m per project to take bio-based feedstock - agricultural and forestry residues, bio-waste, side streams - through biorefining into chemicals, materials, fibres, packaging, food ingredients or crop inputs, from lab proof (TRL 3-5) all the way to a first full industrial-scale biorefinery (TRL 8), with the industry consortium expected to put in its own money as in-kind contribution.
  eligibility   These are Joint Undertaking conditions, and they do not appear in a Commission-run Horizon cluster.

In-kind contribution is scored, not merely recorded. Under Impact, proposals are assessed on their ability to reach the level of in-kind contribution to operational activities set for the topic, expressed as a percentage of total eligible project costs: 5% for research and innovation actions, 15% for innovation actions and 20% for Flagship innovation actions. IKOP means "Contributions by private members, constituent entities or the affiliated entities of either, by international organisations and by contributing partners, consisting of the eligible costs incurred by them in implementing indirect actions less the contribution of that joint undertaking and of the participating states of that joint undertaking to those costs." A consortium carrying no private-member industry cannot generate IKOP and scores badly on Impact.

Private membership must be evidenced in the proposal itself: "Innovation Actions, including Flagships, and Research and Innovation Actions: For all legal entities that are members of the BIC consortium, a certification from BIC attesting to this fact should be included in the proposal". BIC is the Bio-based Industries Consortium, the private member of the Joint Undertaking.

Flagship topics are a separate track with heavier requirements. They require, in a dedicated annex, "a detailed business plan, which should include an estimate of appropriate economic indicators, including Net Present Value (NPV), with all critical underlying assumptions clearly defined and appropriately justified", and "Only for Flagship topics: As part of the panel review, the CBE JU will organise hearings with applicants of all Flagships proposals." Research and innovation actions need only a qualitative business case, and non-Flagship innovation actions a quantified business case and business model.

Feedstock is constrained by origin and by principle. Every research and innovation action, innovation action and Flagship must describe bio-based feedstock within the scope of the Joint Undertaking's strategic research agenda and "use bio-based feedstock that is both produced (or generated) and processed in EU Member States and/or countries associated to Horizon Europe", with evidence on climate, biodiversity, pollution and water. Subject to the food-first and cascading-use principles, "agricultural biomass can be used as feedstock for CBE JU projects". Innovation actions and Flagships must forecast agricultural-biomass volumes and assess interference with the food supply chain.

Life-cycle sustainability assessment is mandatory: an ex-ante environmental assessment for every action type, plus an ex-post dedicated task for research and innovation actions or a work package for innovation actions and Flagships. A multi-actor approach is required for all types of action.

Maturity is set high for deployment. "IAs projects are demonstration activities in relevant and operational environments and expected to deliver TRL 6-8 at the end of the projects. In particular, Flagship projects will need to deliver TRL 8 at the end of the projects." Research and innovation actions deliver TRL 3-5.

Every 2026 grant topic carries an intellectual-property derogation: "The granting authority may, up to 4 years after the end of the action, object to a transfer of ownership or to the exclusive licensing of results", as set out in the specific provision of Annex 5.

==============================================================================
CEFRIEL SOCIETA CONSORTILE A RESPONSABILITA LIMITATA SOCIETA BENEFIT (IT)   regime: horizon_eu_pillar_ii
  rank of the won door in the accessible pool: 16/76
------------------------------------------------------------------------------
WON   horizon_eu_miss
  name          Horizon EU Miss
  schemeCode    (none)   capitalType grant
  body          (none)
  window        (none) ((none))  recurrence (none)
  description   Grants that implement five named EU Missions — Adaptation to Climate Change, Cancer, 100 Climate-Neutral and Smart Cities, Restore our Ocean and Waters, and A Soil Deal for Europe — funding demonstration and replication in real territories, where the cities, regions or living labs are beneficiaries of the grant rather than study subjects.
  eligibility   The Missions work programme covers all five EU Missions — "Adaptation to Climate Change", "Cancer", "100 Climate-Neutral and Smart cities by 2030", "Restore our Ocean and Waters by 2030" and "A Soil Deal for Europe" — plus joint calls between them. Its discriminating conditions are membership conditions: the beneficiary must be, or must come from, a place that has already joined the Mission. The Cities Mission and its joint calls repeatedly require: "At least three cities, represented by a local authority or by an entity with an explicit consent from the local authority, each from a different Member State or Associated Country, must participate as beneficiaries." and "At least one of the three cities must be one of the 112 cities selected for the EU Mission on Climate-Neutral and Smart Cities". A two-city variant exists, and a lead/follower variant: "At least three lead cities, represented by a local authority or by an entity with an explicit consent from the local authority, from different Member States or Associated Countries along with at least three follower cities, must participate ensuring a good geographical balance." Joint Cities/Adaptation topics add a charter test: "Entities from at least four cities, each from a different Member State or Associated Country, must participate as beneficiaries." and "at least one must be a signatory to the Adaptation Mission Charter" — the Charter being the instrument by which, per this document, "more than 325 regional and local authorities have committed to working together to transition faster to a climate resilient Europe." Demonstration geography is also prescribed: "Demonstration activities must take place in the territory of at least 3 different regional or local authorities that host cultural heritage, each established in a different Member State or Associated Country." and, on a cross-border topic, "The demonstration areas must span adjacent regions on both sides of a national border." The Soil Mission carries CL6-style conditions: "Proposals must apply the multi-actor approach. See definition of the multi-actor approach in the introduction to this work programme part." on 9 topic pages, plus a biogeographic constraint — "regions: Alpine or Atlantic, i.e., the majority of the living labs of each proposal must be located in one of these two biogeographical regions." — and living-lab cascade funding: "Beneficiaries may provide financial support to third parties to facilitate active involvement of smaller actors (e.g. land managers and owners such as farmers, SMEs or civil societies) in one or more of the living labs of the project." One Adaptation topic makes cascade funding compulsory and unusually large: "Beneficiaries must provide financial support to third parties. The support to third parties can only be provided in the form of grants. The maximum amount to be granted to each third party is EUR 800,000" — the only 'must' FSTP clause in the part, against 14 'may' clauses.
------------------------------------------------------------------------------
SHOWN horizon_eu_cl5
  name          Horizon EU CL5
  schemeCode    (none)   capitalType grant
  body          (none)
  window        (none) ((none))  recurrence (none)
  description   Collaborative R&D and demonstration grants for decarbonising energy and transport — batteries and energy storage, renewables and grids, renewable fuels, CO2 capture and storage, road/rail/air/waterborne mobility, and the climate science underneath — with each topic naming the technology-readiness band the project must reach by its end.
  eligibility   Cluster 5's leaf-level conditions are written per topic in a Technology Readiness Level row and in geographic derogations that no other cluster carries in this combination. 69 topics state an explicit band — "Activities are expected to achieve TRL 4-5 by the end of the project" through "Activities are expected to achieve TRL 7-8 by the end of the project", each closed by "see General Annex B. Activities may start at any TRL." — and 52 of those 69 require TRL 6 or above, so most CL5 money buys demonstration and first-of-a-kind scale-up, not lab work. Second, and unusually heavy for Pillar II, 61 distinct pages attach an exploitation lock to the grant: "The granting authority may, up to 4 years after the end of the action, object to a transfer of ownership or to the exclusive licensing of results, as set out in the specific provision of Annex 5." (Cluster 6 carries this on 6 pages, Missions on 13.) Third, several topics widen or bend the consortium rule on a sectoral-diplomacy basis. Africa-facing climate topics state "Due to the scope of this topic, legal entities established in all African Union member states are exceptionally eligible for Union funding." together with "At least 30% of the beneficiaries must be legal entities established in the African Union member states". Global-climate topics require that "the consortium must include as beneficiary or associated partner at least three independent legal entities established in three different low or lower/upper-middle-income countries". An EU-India battery topic runs the other way: "The project must include at least one work package for coordinated activities with the linked project awarded by the Ministry of Heavy Industry of the Government of India (MHI). Participation of legal entities established in India, which is a third country under Horizon Europe, is only possible as associated partners." Fourth, demonstration topics can force public bodies into the consortium: on the rural shared-mobility topic, "Each proposal must include pilot demonstrations in at least 4 pilot sites" and "The pilots must be community-driven, carried out under the lead or with the involvement of rural/local/regional public authorities/administrations and the local transport authorities, and these entities must be included in the consortium as beneficiaries." Finally, on ~10 grid, AI-for-energy and connected-vehicle Innovation Action topics: "entities established in an eligible country but which are directly or indirectly controlled by China or by a legal entity established in China are not eligible to participate in the action." Note these are topic-level rules, not a single cluster-wide gate: an applicant must read the topic's own conditions table.

SHOWN horizon_eu_cl1
  name          Horizon EU CL1
  schemeCode    (none)   capitalType grant
  body          (none)
  window        (none) ((none))  recurrence (none)
  description   Collaborative R&I grants for health research that generates clinical evidence — disease prevention and treatment, vaccines and medical countermeasures, health-system transformation, and the tools and cohorts behind them — for consortia that usually run or plan clinical studies.
  eligibility   Cluster 1 Health's discriminating condition runs the opposite way from the security clusters: it widens the eligible-country set rather than narrowing it. On 37 distinct pages the Eligibility conditions row states "In recognition of the opening of the US National Institutes of Health’s programmes to European researchers, any legal entity established in the United States of America is eligible to receive Union funding." On Coordination and Support Action topics the same reciprocity is granted in a narrower form — US entities "may exceptionally participate as a beneficiary or affiliated entity, and are eligible to receive Union funding" — paired with a derogation from the standard consortium rule in the other direction: "Coordinators of projects must be legal entities established in an EU Member State or Associated Country." No other Pillar II cluster opens funding eligibility to US entities as a standing rule. Second, the cluster is organised around clinical evidence generation and defines its own unusually broad object: "In the context of the Health Cluster Work Programme part for 2026-2027, a clinical study covers clinical studies/trials/investigations/cohorts and is defined as any systematic prospective or retrospective collection and analysis of health data obtained from individual patients or healthy persons" — explicitly wider than Regulation 536/2014, and topics repeatedly note that "As proposals under this topic are expected to include clinical studies, the use of the template is strongly encouraged", the template being a dedicated clinical-studies annex in the submission system. Third, many topics carry a binding SSH requirement: "This topic requires the effective contribution of social sciences and humanities (SSH) disciplines and the involvement of SSH experts". Fourth, the co-funded European Partnership topics derogate from the standard funding rates: "The funding rate is 50% of the eligible costs." (Rare Diseases) and "The funding rate is 30% of the eligible costs." (Personalised Medicine). Notably, unlike Clusters 3 and 4, this part states no TRL in any topic conditions table (zero TRL rows across 211 pages), and Article 22(5) participation limits appear on only one topic (page 173), so an applicant's country eligibility here is broader than in the security or space clusters, not tighter.

==============================================================================
STICHTING DELTARES (NL)   regime: horizon_eu_pillar_ii
  rank of the won door in the accessible pool: 34/53
------------------------------------------------------------------------------
WON   horizon_eu_miss
  name          Horizon EU Miss
  schemeCode    (none)   capitalType grant
  body          (none)
  window        (none) ((none))  recurrence (none)
  description   Grants that implement five named EU Missions — Adaptation to Climate Change, Cancer, 100 Climate-Neutral and Smart Cities, Restore our Ocean and Waters, and A Soil Deal for Europe — funding demonstration and replication in real territories, where the cities, regions or living labs are beneficiaries of the grant rather than study subjects.
  eligibility   The Missions work programme covers all five EU Missions — "Adaptation to Climate Change", "Cancer", "100 Climate-Neutral and Smart cities by 2030", "Restore our Ocean and Waters by 2030" and "A Soil Deal for Europe" — plus joint calls between them. Its discriminating conditions are membership conditions: the beneficiary must be, or must come from, a place that has already joined the Mission. The Cities Mission and its joint calls repeatedly require: "At least three cities, represented by a local authority or by an entity with an explicit consent from the local authority, each from a different Member State or Associated Country, must participate as beneficiaries." and "At least one of the three cities must be one of the 112 cities selected for the EU Mission on Climate-Neutral and Smart Cities". A two-city variant exists, and a lead/follower variant: "At least three lead cities, represented by a local authority or by an entity with an explicit consent from the local authority, from different Member States or Associated Countries along with at least three follower cities, must participate ensuring a good geographical balance." Joint Cities/Adaptation topics add a charter test: "Entities from at least four cities, each from a different Member State or Associated Country, must participate as beneficiaries." and "at least one must be a signatory to the Adaptation Mission Charter" — the Charter being the instrument by which, per this document, "more than 325 regional and local authorities have committed to working together to transition faster to a climate resilient Europe." Demonstration geography is also prescribed: "Demonstration activities must take place in the territory of at least 3 different regional or local authorities that host cultural heritage, each established in a different Member State or Associated Country." and, on a cross-border topic, "The demonstration areas must span adjacent regions on both sides of a national border." The Soil Mission carries CL6-style conditions: "Proposals must apply the multi-actor approach. See definition of the multi-actor approach in the introduction to this work programme part." on 9 topic pages, plus a biogeographic constraint — "regions: Alpine or Atlantic, i.e., the majority of the living labs of each proposal must be located in one of these two biogeographical regions." — and living-lab cascade funding: "Beneficiaries may provide financial support to third parties to facilitate active involvement of smaller actors (e.g. land managers and owners such as farmers, SMEs or civil societies) in one or more of the living labs of the project." One Adaptation topic makes cascade funding compulsory and unusually large: "Beneficiaries must provide financial support to third parties. The support to third parties can only be provided in the form of grants. The maximum amount to be granted to each third party is EUR 800,000" — the only 'must' FSTP clause in the part, against 14 'may' clauses.
------------------------------------------------------------------------------
SHOWN horizon_eu_cl5
  name          Horizon EU CL5
  schemeCode    (none)   capitalType grant
  body          (none)
  window        (none) ((none))  recurrence (none)
  description   Collaborative R&D and demonstration grants for decarbonising energy and transport — batteries and energy storage, renewables and grids, renewable fuels, CO2 capture and storage, road/rail/air/waterborne mobility, and the climate science underneath — with each topic naming the technology-readiness band the project must reach by its end.
  eligibility   Cluster 5's leaf-level conditions are written per topic in a Technology Readiness Level row and in geographic derogations that no other cluster carries in this combination. 69 topics state an explicit band — "Activities are expected to achieve TRL 4-5 by the end of the project" through "Activities are expected to achieve TRL 7-8 by the end of the project", each closed by "see General Annex B. Activities may start at any TRL." — and 52 of those 69 require TRL 6 or above, so most CL5 money buys demonstration and first-of-a-kind scale-up, not lab work. Second, and unusually heavy for Pillar II, 61 distinct pages attach an exploitation lock to the grant: "The granting authority may, up to 4 years after the end of the action, object to a transfer of ownership or to the exclusive licensing of results, as set out in the specific provision of Annex 5." (Cluster 6 carries this on 6 pages, Missions on 13.) Third, several topics widen or bend the consortium rule on a sectoral-diplomacy basis. Africa-facing climate topics state "Due to the scope of this topic, legal entities established in all African Union member states are exceptionally eligible for Union funding." together with "At least 30% of the beneficiaries must be legal entities established in the African Union member states". Global-climate topics require that "the consortium must include as beneficiary or associated partner at least three independent legal entities established in three different low or lower/upper-middle-income countries". An EU-India battery topic runs the other way: "The project must include at least one work package for coordinated activities with the linked project awarded by the Ministry of Heavy Industry of the Government of India (MHI). Participation of legal entities established in India, which is a third country under Horizon Europe, is only possible as associated partners." Fourth, demonstration topics can force public bodies into the consortium: on the rural shared-mobility topic, "Each proposal must include pilot demonstrations in at least 4 pilot sites" and "The pilots must be community-driven, carried out under the lead or with the involvement of rural/local/regional public authorities/administrations and the local transport authorities, and these entities must be included in the consortium as beneficiaries." Finally, on ~10 grid, AI-for-energy and connected-vehicle Innovation Action topics: "entities established in an eligible country but which are directly or indirectly controlled by China or by a legal entity established in China are not eligible to participate in the action." Note these are topic-level rules, not a single cluster-wide gate: an applicant must read the topic's own conditions table.

STICHTING HOGESCHOOL VAN AMSTERDAM (NL)   regime: horizon_eu_pillar_ii
  rank of the won door in the accessible pool: 33/52
------------------------------------------------------------------------------
WON   horizon_eu_cl4
  name          Horizon EU CL4
  schemeCode    (none)   capitalType grant
  body          HaDEA
  window        (none) ((none))  recurrence (none)
  description   Collaborative R&I and innovation grants for the EU's technology sovereignty stack — AI, quantum, photonics, semiconductors, advanced materials and manufacturing, circular industry, raw materials and space — where eligibility is gated on where the applicant is established and who ultimately controls it.
  eligibility   Cluster 4's discriminating condition is a per-topic patchwork of bespoke eligible-country lists invoked under Article 22(5), replacing the standard Member State or Associated Country test with something narrower or wider depending on the technology. Space topics are the tightest: "In order to achieve the expected outcomes, and safeguard the Union’s strategic assets, interests, autonomy, or security, participation is limited to legal entities established in Member States, Norway and Iceland." One EU Space Surveillance and Tracking topic goes further: "participation is limited to legal entities established in Member States. Proposals including entities established in countries outside the scope specified in the call/topic/action will be ineligible." Digital and semiconductor topics use an intermediate list — "participation is limited to legal entities established in Member States, Iceland and Norway and the following additional associated countries: Canada, Israel, Republic of Korea, New Zealand, Switzerland, and the United Kingdom." Raw-materials topics widen it instead: "participation in this topic is limited to legal entities established in Member States, associated countries, OECD countries, African Union Member States, MERCOSUR, CARIFORUM, Andean Community and countries with which the EU has concluded strategic partnerships on raw materials". On restricted topics an ownership test also applies: "entities established in an eligible country listed above, but which are directly or indirectly controlled by a non-eligible country or by a non-eligible country entity, may not participate in the action unless it can be demonstrated, by means of guarantees positively assessed by their eligible country of establishment". Second, space topics use a different TRL yardstick entirely: "The reference TRL definition is the ISO 16290:2013 applicable to the space sector." Third, the cluster derogates from the standard consortium rule in both directions: Fast Track to Research and Innovation topics impose a ceiling — "The following additional eligibility criteria apply: consortia may include a maximum of 6 independent eligible entities as participants." — while a quantum inertial-navigation topic drops the three-entity floor entirely: "The following additional eligibility criteria apply: Proposals must be submitted by a single legal entity (mono-beneficiary CSA) wich is an SME." Fourth, one space topic derogates from the standard IA rate: "This is an Innovation Action (IA) with reduced funding rate (45%)." A space-security topic also imports the Cluster 3 practitioner mechanic, requiring "participation, as beneficiaries, of at least two crisis or security practitioner 190 organisations or agencies, established in at least two different EU Member States or Associated Countries."
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

TECHNISCHE UNIVERSITEIT DELFT (NL)   regime: horizon_eu_pillar_ii
  rank of the won door in the accessible pool: 11/45
------------------------------------------------------------------------------
WON   horizon_eu_cl3
  name          Horizon EU CL3
  schemeCode    (none)   capitalType grant
  body          (none)
  window        (none) ((none))  recurrence (none)
  description   Collaborative R&I and innovation grants for civil security technology — fighting crime and terrorism, border and customs control, disaster resilience, infrastructure protection and cybersecurity — where the consortium must include the public authorities who would actually operate the result.
  eligibility   The discriminating condition in Cluster 3 is a mandatory end-user beneficiary quota written into almost every topic's Eligibility conditions row under "The following additional eligibility conditions apply:". Typical wording: "This topic requires the active involvement, as beneficiaries, of at least 3 Police Authorities 47 from at least 3 different EU Member States or Associated Countries." The definition is deliberately narrow: ‘Police Authorities’ means "public authorities explicitly designated by national law, or other entities legally mandated by the competent national authority, for the prevention, detection and/or investigation of terrorist offences or other criminal offences, specifically excluding police academies, forensic institutes, training facilities as well as border and customs authorities". The required practitioner class tracks the destination: Police Authorities (2 or 3, on many topics alongside at least 2 Civil Society Organisations or NGOs) for Fighting Crime and Terrorism; "at least 2 Border or Coast Guard Authorities from at least 2 different EU Member States or Associated Countries" for Border Management; "at least 2 Customs Authorities from at least 2 different EU Member States or Associated Countries"; first responders, medical emergency authorities or authorities in charge of disaster risk for Disaster-Resilient Society; and on PCP/PPI topics "practitioners and 3 public procurers. These beneficiaries must be from at least 3 different EU Member States or Associated Countries." Named practitioners must be declared in the "Information about security practitioners" table in the application form. Second, every topic states a TRL band in its conditions table (40 such rows in this part, ranging TRL 4 to TRL 8), e.g. "Activities are expected to achieve TRL 6-8 by the end of the project"; the Health work programme states none. Third, on innovation actions in critical technology areas the cluster activates the General Annex control test: "entities established in an eligible country but which are directly or indirectly controlled by China or by a legal entity established in China are not eligible to participate in the action" (16 topic tables). Fourth, topic tables carry a Security Sensitive Topics row on classified background and EUCI/SEN results — "Please refer to the related provisions in section B Security — EU classified and sensitive information of the General Annexes." Fifth, the 2026 cybersecurity budget is run by a delegated body: "The Commission intends to conclude a contribution agreement with the European Cybersecurity Competence Centre (ECCC)", whose call is limited under Article 22(5) so that participation "is limited to legal entities established in Member States and Associated Countries" with entities controlled by a non-eligible country excluded outright.
------------------------------------------------------------------------------
SHOWN horizon_eu_cl6
  name          Horizon EU CL6
  schemeCode    (none)   capitalType grant
  body          (none)
  window        (none) ((none))  recurrence (none)
  description   Collaborative research and innovation grants for food systems, farming, forestry, fisheries and aquaculture, biodiversity, soil, water, the circular bioeconomy and rural/coastal/urban communities — where on most topics farmers, foresters, fishers, advisors and other end-users must sit inside the consortium as co-creators, not as an advisory board.
  eligibility   Cluster 6's discriminating condition is the multi-actor approach, and it is an eligibility condition, not an ambition. 40 of the 69 topic conditions tables carry "The proposals must apply the multi-actor approach. See definition of the multi-actor approach in this work programme part." The definition, set out in a dedicated section 'Specific conditions for multi-actor projects', binds the consortium's composition and method: "A multi-actor project ensures the genuine and sufficient involvement of a targeted array of actors in co-creation, which serves the objectives of the project proposal." Topic texts translate this into named practitioner classes: "Proposals must implement the multi-actor approach, with a consortium based on a balanced mix of relevant actors with complementary knowledge clearly activating advisors, farmers and/or foresters and/or rural actors." It also imposes a deliverable no sibling cluster requires: "the new knowledge and innovation generated by the multi-actor projects must be summarised in an appropriate number of ‘practice abstracts’ in the common EIP-AGRI format for Horizon". Second, a set of place-based topics require named territorial demonstrators inside the consortium as beneficiaries. Living-lab topics: "at least five (5) distinct ‘living labs’ linked to regional or local authorities (and possibly together with their public and/or private ecosystem actors operating in their respective territories) must be part of the consortium as beneficiaries, among which at least two (2) living labs must be from different demonstration sites and another three (3) from other and different replication sites. 1 out of the 3 replication sites must be located in Horizon Europe widening countries (including Outermost Regions)." A rural/urban replication topic goes further: "at least nine (9) distinct regional or local authorities must be part of the consortium as beneficiaries, among which at least three (3) from different demonstration cities/regions and another six (6) from other and different replication cities/regions." Third, geographic derogations are common: "due to the specific challenge of this topic, in addition to the minimum number of participants set out in the General Annexes, consortia must include at least three independent legal entities established in an African Union member state"; "Due to the scope of this topic, legal entities established in Brazil are exceptionally eligible for Union funding."; "All international organisations are exceptionally eligible for funding."; and on one topic "the participation of at least one research institution or relevant public authority from Ukraine in the consortium is mandatory." Fourth, two partnership-continuation topics collapse the consortium rule to a single named applicant, e.g. "The proposal must be submitted by the coordinator of the consortium funded under HORIZON-CL6-2022-GOVERNANCE-01-02 and HORIZON-CL6-2024-GOVERNANCE-01-1: European Partnership for a climate neutral, sustainable and productive Blue Economy." These are effectively closed to new entrants.
